Potential approaches:
1. Iterative LP / Frank-Wolfe from SOTA. Implement efficiently: Given a, M, find g maximizing sum subject to a*g≤M, g≥0. Constraints are 2n-1, variables n. Or use scipy.optimize.linprog? Need efficient repeated. But maybe one or few LP solves enough? Let's explore structure.

LP: max ∑ g_j s.t. for each k, (A g)_k = ∑_{j} a_{k-j} g_j ≤ M; g_j≥0. Matrix is banded? Actually each column a_{k-j} for k around j; nonzeros 2n-1 per column (convolution full), dense banded? For variable j, it appears in constraints k=j+r for r=0..2j or? Givens 0≤j≤n-1; k from j to 2n-2-j? length 2min(j+1,n-j?)? It's banded but wide near center. Total nonzeros ~2n^2. For n=10000, 200M too big. But n maybe 6962, 100M. LP heavy.

But maybe the LP optimum has simple form? For fixed a, constraints are weighted sums. We maximize sum g; dual: minimize M y? Let's derive. Primal max 1^T g, A g ≤ M 1, g≥0. Dual min M ∑_{k} y_k s.t. ∑_{k: k=j+r} y_k a_{k-j} ≥ 1, y_k≥0. So dual variables y_k assigned to bins. At optimum, complementary slackness. We need primal sum. If we set y_k uniform? constraint for j: a_j ∑_{r} y_{j+r}? Need.

import time, math
import numpy as np

def safe_evaluate(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    if not np.isfinite(val): return float('inf')
    return float(val)
```
But evaluate_sequence may raise if sequence huge? We'll try.

`fft_convolution(a)`:
```
def fft_convolution(a):
    n = a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 128:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    m = 1
    need = 2*n -1
    while m < need: m <<=1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    return np.fft.irfft(F * F, m)[:need]
```
Need ensure F*F (not conj) works. For real a, irfft of F^2 should produce real convolution. Let's test with a=[1,2], m=4. F = [3, 1-2i, -1, 1+2i]; F² [9, -3-4i, 1, -3+4i]; irFFT length4 inverse gives? Known convolution [1,4,4]. Let's compute quickly: inverse at k: (1/4) sum. Might work. We'll use full `np.fft.fft` and `ifft` to be safe: `F=np.fft.fft(a,m); c=np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:need]`. For m power2. More memory but okay. Use complex. For signed negatives, same.

`fast_score(a)`:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if not finite or abs(s)<1e-300: return inf
    c=fft_convolution(a)
    maxc=c.max()
    if not finite: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*maxc/(s*s))
```
For signed, if maxc negative? c0 square ensures nonnegative; but FFT error could -1e-16, max maybe positive. If all negative impossible. If maxc<0 due a all zero, handled. For nonnegative, use maxc.

`normalize_max(a)`:
```
def normalize_max(a):
    c=fft_convolution(a); m=c.max()
    if m<=0 or not finite: return a, inf, -1, c
    b=a/m
    s=b.sum()
    score=2*n/(s*s)
    return b, score, s
```
Actually if a has sum zero but max>0 (signed alternating sum zero), evaluator would inf; fast_score inf. For LP, need nonnegative sum positive.

LP function:
```
def lp_direction(a, M, deadline=None):
    n=len(a)
    if n<2: return None
    # build A row wise
    A = np.empty((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       if js<=je:
          # need a[k-je : k-js-1 : -1]
          A[k, js:je+1] = a[k-je : k-js-1 : -1]
    # linprog min -1 g
    res=linprog(c=-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1,M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
```
But `M` should be 1 if normalized max. For numerical, use `M+1e-12`. b_ub small. A coefficients can be zero? positive. If a has negative (not for nonneg), LP maybe unbounded; check res.status.
Res returns g=res.x. G=g.sum(). If G <= direction sum? Return g.
Potential issue: `linprog` with many constraints maybe status 4 numerical. Use `method='highs'`. If scipy version older default hiGHS? Available maybe. If not, fallback to None.
Time: building A loops O(n²); for n=2048 ~4M assignments, okay. For multiple lengths maybe heavy. We'll limit.

`solve_lp_direction(a, deadline)`:
```
def solve_lp_direction(a):
    n=a.size
    if n<20 or n>2048: return None
    # build dense A
    A = np.empty((2*n-1, n), float)
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       js = max(0, k - n + 1)
       je = min(n - 1, k)
       if js <= je:
          # length L = je-js+1
          # a indices k-je down to k-js
          A[k, js:je+1] = a[k-je : k-js-1 : -1]
    try:
       res = linprog(-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(2*n-1), bounds=(0, None), method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res.status in (0, 3)?? status 0 optimal, 3 max iter? If 3 maybe x feasible? Use if optimal.
    g=res.x
    g=np.clip(g,0,None)
    return g
```
Potential issue: `A` for k row may be all zeros for some? No, every k has at least one variable. But A is dense? We fill slices; rows outside slice remain zeros (good). Memory initialization empty; ensure all entries assigned? For each row, slice assigned; slice length equals number nonzeros. Good.
- The constraint `a*g≤1`: For k=n-1 central row, js=0,je=n-1, assign a[n-1:-1:-1], OK. For k=2n-2, js=n-1,je=n-1, assign a[0:0:-1]? Let's test: k=2n-2, js=je=n-1. `a[k-je : k-js-1 : -1]` = a[n-2 : -1 : -1]? Wait k-je=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1; k-js-1=2n-2-(n-1)-1=n-2. Slice a[n-1:n-2:-1] gives a[n-1]. Good. For k=0: js=je=0, a[0:-1:-1] gives a[0]. Good. For js >0 e.g., k=n, js=1, je=n-1. Need coefficients a[n-1],a[n-2],...,a[0]? k-je=1, k-js-1=0. Slice a[1:0:-1] gives a[1]? Wait missing a0? Let's recalc: k=n, valid j=1..n-1, coeff a_{n-j}: for j=1 ->a_{n-1}, j=n-1 ->a_1? But j=n? no index n invalid. Infinite c_n missing a0 a_n and a_n a0; finite sum excludes j=0 and j=n, so coefficient for j=n-1 is a_1, not a0. So slice a[1:0:-1] gives a[1]? Correct. Good. For k=n-1: need j=0..n-1 coeff a[n-1]...a[0], slice a[n-1:-1:-1] correct.

But `linprog` A_ub expects shape (m,n); with n large, passing dense may be slow. We can create sparse `coo` to reduce memory? Dense 2M for n=1024 fine. For 2048 dense 8M? 4095*2048=8.3M, 66MB, okay. For 4096 33M, 264MB maybe high. Cap 2048.

Iteration plan:
```
def optimize_nonnegative_length(n, deadline):
   a=make_q(n); a,_ = normalize_max(a) # q max already 1, sum S
   best=a; best_score=2*n/(S*S)
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time remaining <60: break
      g=solve_lp_direction(a) # with M=1
      if g is None: break
      G=g.sum(); S=a.sum()
      if G <= S*1.0001: break
      gc=(S/G)*g
      newa, newscore = line_search(a, gc)
      if newscore < best_score:
         a, newscore, _ = normalize_max(newa)
         best=...; update safe_evaluate maybe every few
      else:
         # maybe small step? if t=0 no; break? full line search best maybe 0; try random block
   # block refinement
   return best sequence scale sum1, best_score
```
- LP from q: first iteration may produce direction; subsequent states not exactly M=1? normalize.
- The LP constraints use M=1. If current a max1. If linprog solution G maybe not >S after first, stop.
- Need exact evaluate for global best: after each accepted `safe_evaluate(a.tolist())` maybe expensive but n small. Use fast_score for selection; exact at end for best. But global compare with other candidates should use exact to avoid FFT error? FFT good. We can update global with fast and final exact check all candidates? We'll keep `best_exact_score` and evaluate exact for candidates when accept or at end of each length. Since n<=2048, evaluate direct O4M, a few times okay. Use `safe_evaluate`.

Block refinement nonnegative:
```
def refine_blocks(a, score, rng, deadline, iters=...):
   n=a.size
   best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   # scale state for fast_score (scale invariant) maybe keep as is.
   while time<deadline:
      for _ in range(batch):
         cand=best.copy()
         width = rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//4))
         start = rng.integers(0, n-width)
         factor = rng.normal(1, 0.2)
         cand[start:start+width] *= factor
         if rng.random()<0.3: cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
         cand = np.clip(cand,0,1000)
         sc=fast_score(cand)
         if sc < best_score -1e-12:
             best=cand; best_score=sc; # maybe normalize max
      # try larger random block
   return best,best_score
```
But multiplying blocks can break q low constraints; maybe small steps. Use `factor = exp(rng.normal(0, 0.1))`. If no improvement for many, increase noise? We'll keep simple.

```
def generate_signed_candidates(n, rng, deadline):
   best=None
   for restart in range(5):
      a = np.ones(n)/n  # sum1
      # add random low-budget? Use random normal zero mean with amplitude 0.5
      b = rng.normal(0,0.5,n)
      b -= b.mean()
      a = 1.0/n + b
      a -= a.mean() - 1/n
      # maybe enforce positivity? no
      # subgradient descent
      adam_m=np.zeros(n); adam_v=np.zeros(n)
      lr=0.05; beta1=0.9; beta2=0.99; tau=0.05/n? Need tune.
      for it in range(2000):
         c=fft_convolution(a)
         maxc=c.max()
         # smooth weights using top? Could use hard max subgradient: active = c >= maxc - eps. Use weights uniform over active for stability. For signed, max c includes c0; if c0 has max, gradient =2a0. Hard may be okay.
         eps=1e-6*max(1,abs(maxc))
         w = (c >= maxc - eps).astype(float); if sum==0: w = softmax(c/tau)
         w /= w.sum()
         grad_a = 2*np.correlate(w, a, mode='valid')  # length n? Need check W length 2n-1.
         # project to sum constraint: grad_a -= a.dot(grad_a) (since sum=1, tangent projection subtract mean dot)
         grad_a -= np.dot(a, grad_a)
         # Adam
         ...
         a = a + step*grad_a? Minimize, so a -= lr*m/(sqrt(v)+1e-8)
         a -= a.mean() - 1/n; if a.sum()<0 flip? sum fixed.
         score=fast_score(a)
      best min
```
Need ensure `np.correlate` with W length 2n-1 and a length n returns length n? `np.correlate(full, a, mode='valid')`: if full longer, valid length = len(full)-len(a)+1 = n. It returns out_k = ∑_{i=0}^{len(a)-1} full[i+k] a[i]? I think yes. Let's test small: full=[w0,w1,w2], a=[a0,a1]. correlate(full,a,'valid') maybe [full0*a0 + full1*a1, full1*a0 + full2*a1]. We need i=0: w0 a0 + w1 a1? Our grad_a0 = 2∑_{j=0}^{1} W_{0+j} a_j = W0 a0 + W1 a1. Yes matches. For i=1: W1 a0 + W2 a1? Our grad_a1 = j=0 W1 a0 + j=1 W2 a1. Yes. Good. def _q_sequence(n):
    a=np.empty(n,float)
    if n>0:
       a[0]=1.0
       for i in range(1,n): a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)

def scan_q(max_n):
    best_n=1; best_score=2.0
    a=np.empty(max_n,float); a[0]=1; S=1
    for n in range(1,max_n+1):
       if n>1: a[n-1]=a[n-2]*(2*(n-1)-1)/(2*(n-1))
       S += a[n-1] if n>1 else 1?
       score=2*n/(S*S)
       if score<best_score: best_score=score; best_n=n
    return best_n, best_score
```

`convolution_full(x,y)`:
```
def convolution_full(x,y):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); y=np.asarray(y,float)
    nx=x.size; ny=y.size
    need=nx+ny-1
    if nx==0 or ny==0: return np.empty(0)
    if min(nx,ny) <= 64 or need <= 256:
       return np.convolve(x,y)
    m=1
    while m<need: m<<=1
    F=np.fft.fft(x, m)
    G=np.fft.fft(y, m)
    return np.fft.ifft(F*G).real[:need]
```
For same use N²? Use complex. For n=2048, m=4096, fine. If signed, real part.

`optimize_nonnegative(n, deadline)`:
```
def optimize_nonnegative(n, deadline):
    a=_q_sequence(n)
    a, score, S = normalize_state(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    max_iters = 8 if n<=512 else 4
    use_lp = linprog is not None and n<=1536
    if use_lp:
      for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
        g=lp_direction(a)
        if g is None: break
        G=g.sum()
        S=a.sum()
        if G <= S*1.001: break
        b=(S/G)*g
        # optional clip
        new, sc = line_search_state(a,b)
        # normalize new
        new, sc_norm, Snew = normalize_state(new)
        if sc_norm < best_score - 1e-12:
           best=new; best_score=sc_norm; a=best
        else:
           # if no improvement, maybe break LP loop
           break
    # block refinement
    best,best_score = refine_blocks(best,best_score, deadline-30)
    return best,best_score
```

Implement block refinement:
```
def refine_blocks(a, score, deadline, seed=123):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=a.size
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    # Ensure scale max1? We can use as is.
    no_improve=0
    step=0.05
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       for _ in range(50):
          cand=best.copy()
          width=int(rng.integers(max(1, n//100), max(2, n//4)+1))
          start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
          # multiplicative lognormal
          f=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05+step, width))
          cand[start:start+width] *= f
          if rng.random() < 0.25:
             cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
          cand=np.clip(cand,0.0,1000.0)
          if not np.any(cand): continue
          sc=fast_score(cand)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
              best=cand; best_score=sc; no_improve=0
              # rescale to max1 to keep numbers
              best, best_score, _ = normalize_state(best)
          else:
              no_improve += 1
       if no_improve > 1000:
          step=min(0.5, step*1.5)
          no_improve=0
          if step>0.4: break? 
    return best,best_score
```
Multiplicative can increase sum and max; normalize_state after accept changes best_score? Score invariant under scale, but if fast_score before normalization differs? Normalizing max to1 actually `normalize_state` returns score computed; should be same within rounding. If after normalization score slightly worse due rounding? It is same. But it overrides best_score with normalized score; okay. If normalization changes sequence, accept condition based on unnormalized fast_score; score invariant so fine.
Potential issue: normalizing after each accept may disrupt random state? ok.

But block multiplicative around q likely won't improve because any deviation from active constraints reduces? It might find small improvements if noise. Could also use "mirror" to exploit symmetry. Fine.

def signed_candidate(n, rng, deadline):
    a = np.ones(n); # sum not fixed? scale later
    b = rng.normal(0,0.2,n); b -= b.mean(); a += b; a /= a.sum() # sum1

    best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a)

       z=(c - maxc)/tau

def make_valid_list(a, nonnegative=True, target_sum=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.ndim!=1 or a.size==0: return [1.0]
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    if nonnegative:
       a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    else:
       a=np.clip(a,-1000.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or abs(s)<0.01:
       if nonnegative:
          a=1.0*a/ max(a.size,1) # sum ~1
       else:
          a += 1.0/a.size
    # scale to sum target if sum nonzero
    if abs(s)>1e-12:
       a = target_sum * a / s
    if nonnegative: a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    else: a=np.clip(a,-1000,1000)
    # ensure sum >=0.01 after scale? target sum 1
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

Pseudo:
```
def signed_smooth_candidate(n, rng, deadline):
    best=None; best_score=inf
    for restart in range(8):
       if time>deadline: break
       a = np.ones(n)/n
       # add random mixture of sinusoids to get low autocorr? b=sin(random phase)+...
       b = 0
       for _ in range(4): freq = rng.uniform(1, n/2); phase=rng.uniform(0,2π); b += np.sin(2π*freq*np.arange(n)/n)
       b -= b.mean()
       scale = 0.5 / (np.linalg.norm(b) + 1e-12) * np.mean(np.abs(a))
       a = a + scale*b
       a -= a.mean() - 1/n
       m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); tau=0.2/n
       for it in range(3000):
          if time>deadline: break
          c=convolution_full(a,a)
          maxc=np.max(c)
          if not finite or maxc<=0: break
          z=(c-maxc)/tau
          z=z - np.max(z)
          w=np.exp(z); wsum=w.sum()
          if wsum<=0 or not finite: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
          else w/=wsum
          grad=2.0*np.correlate(w, a, mode='valid')
          if grad.size != n: break
          grad -= np.dot(a, grad)
          m = 0.9*m + 0.1*grad
          v = 0.99*v + 0.01*grad*grad
          step = 0.5 * np.mean(np.abs(a)) / (np.sqrt(v + 1e-20) + 1e-12) # elementwise? This yields array step; maybe too large.
          newa = a - 0.1 * m / (np.sqrt(v)+1e-12)  # use constant 0.1 relative?
          # Better: newa = a - m/(sqrt(v)+eps) * (0.2*np.mean(abs(a))/(np.mean(np.abs(m/(...)))+eps))
          denom=np.sqrt(v)+1e-12
          delta = m/denom
          scale = 0.2*np.mean(np.abs(a)) / (np.mean(np.abs(delta))+1e-12)
          newa = a - min(scale,10)*delta
          newa -= np.mean(newa) - 1/n
          sc=fast_score(newa)
          if sc < best_score:
             best=newa.copy(); best_score=sc
          tau *= 0.98
    return best, best_score
```

Detailed functions:

```
def _next_pow2(x):
    m=1
    while m<x: m <<=1
    return m
```

`convolution_full(x,y)`:
```
def convolution_full(x, y):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    nx = x.size; ny = y.size
    if nx == 0 or ny == 0: return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    need = nx + ny - 1
    if need <= 128:
        return np.convolve(x, y)
    m = 1
    while m < need: m <<= 1
    fx = np.fft.fft(x, m)
    fy = np.fft.fft(y, m)
    out = np.fft.ifft(fx * fy).real[:need]
    return out
```
For self convolution, using two transforms same; could optimize but okay. For large n 2048, 2 FFT + iFFT each. For line search many precompute maybe okay.

`fast_score(a)`:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0: return inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return inf
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or abs(s) < 1e-300: return inf
    c = convolution_full(a, a)
    if c.size == 0: return inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        if m < 0.0:
            m = 0.0
        else: return inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))
```
For signed, max can be 0 if all zero; s zero handled. If max negative impossible but due numerical maybe set 0.

`normalize_state(a)`:
```
def normalize_state(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n <=0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return a, inf, 0
    c=convolution_full(a,a)
    m=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else inf
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 1e-300:
        return a, inf, a.sum()
    b = a / m
    s = float(b.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or abs(s) < 1e-300:
       return b, inf, s
    score = float(2.0 * n / (s*s))
    return b, score, s
```
If b values at end maybe tiny. For nonnegative, b nonneg.

`line_search_state(a,b)`:
```
def _line_value(t, caa, cab, cbb, n, inv_s2):
    u=1.0-t
    c = (u*u)*caa + (t*u)*cab + (t*t)*cbb
    mv = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mv): return inf
    if mv < 0: mv=0.0
    return 2.0*n*mv*inv_s2

def line_search_state(a,b):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); b=np.asarray(b,float)
    n=a.size
    sa=a.sum(); sb=b.sum()
    if sa<=0 or sb<=0: return a, fast_score(a)
    b = (sa/sb)*b
    caa=convolution_full(a,a); cab=convolution_full(a,b); cbb=convolution_full(b,b)
    inv=1/(sa*sa)
    lo=0.0; hi=1.0
    best_t=0.0; best_f=fast_score(a)
    for t in (0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1):
       f=_line_value(...)
       if f<best_f: best_f=f; best_t=t
    # ternary
    for _ in range(40):
       m1=lo+(hi-lo)/3; m2=hi-(hi-lo)/3
       f1=_line_value(m1,...); f2=_line_value(m2,...)
       if f1 < f2: hi=m2
       else: lo=m1
    t=0.5*(lo+hi); f=_line_value(t,...)
    if f<best_f: best_f=f; best_t=t
    cand = (1-best_t)*a + best_t*b
    cand = np.where(np.isfinite(cand), cand, a)
    return cand, best_f
```
But the ternary may miss endpoints? It searches [0,1]; included endpoints initial. Good.

SciPy accepts tuple as bounds for all? Yes `bounds=(0, None)` works. Use upper 1e6 to help feasibility. If true opt >1e6, not likely. Use tuple? In linprog, bounds can be sequence or (min, max) applied to all? Docs: if tuple, min and max same? For variable bounds, `bounds=(0, None)` may be interpreted as bounds for all? ```

```
def optimize_nonnegative(n, deadline):
    a = q_sequence(n)
    a, score, s = normalize_state(a)
    if not finite score: return a, score
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    if linprog is not None and 16 <= n <= 1536:
       max_iter = 6 if n <= 512 else 3
       for it in range(max_iter):
          if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
          g = lp_direction(a)
          if g is None: break
          G = float(g.sum()); S = float(a.sum())
          if G <= S * 1.0005: break
          b = (S / G) * g
          new, sc = line_search_state(a,b)
          if new.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(new)): break
          new = np.clip(new, 0.0, None)
          new, sc_norm, _ = normalize_state(new)
          if np.isfinite(sc_norm) and sc_norm < best_score - 1e-12:
              best = new.copy(); best_score = sc_norm; a = best
              print(...)
          else:
              break
    # block refinement
    remain = deadline - 20
    if remain > time.monotonic() + 2:
       best, best_score = refine_blocks(best, best_score, remain, seed=1)
    return best, best_score
```

In block refinement, if no improvements for long, break. We'll set max outer loops while `time < deadline` and `attempts < max_attempts` maybe. `attempts` increments per inner batch. If no_improve grows >2000 break.

```
def refine_blocks(a, score, deadline, seed=12345):
    if deadline <= time.monotonic()+5: return a,score
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=a.size
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    step=0.05
    no_improve=0
    attempts=0
    max_attempts=1000 if n>1000 else 3000
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and attempts < max_attempts:
       width_min=max(1, n//200)
       width_max=max(width_min+1, n//8)
       for _ in range(10):
          ar=best.copy()
          width=int(rng.integers(width_min, min(width_max, n)+1))
          st=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
          f=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, step, width))
          ar[st:st+width] *= f
          if rng.random() < 0.25: ar = 0.5*(ar + ar[::-1])
          ar = np.clip(ar, 0.0, 1000.0)
          sc = fast_score(ar)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
             ar, sc, _ = normalize_state(ar)
             ar = np.clip(ar,0,1000)
             sc = fast_score(ar)
             if sc < best_score -1e-12:
                best=ar; best_score=sc; no_improve=0
             else: no_improve+=1
          else:
             no_improve +=1
          attempts +=1
          if attempts >= max_attempts or time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       if no_improve > 2000:
          step = min(0.6, step*1.4)
          no_improve=0
       if step > 0.5: break
    return best, best_score
```
Block perturb around q maybe not improve; but okay.

`scan_q(max_n=4096)`:
```
def scan_q(max_n=4096):
    if max_n <1: return 1,2.0
    a=np.empty(max_n,float); a[0]=1.0; S=1.0
    best_n=1; best=2.0
    for n in range(1, max_n+1):
       if n>1:
          idx=n-1; a[idx]=a[idx-1]*(2*idx-1)/(2*idx)
          S += a[idx]
       else: S= a[0]
       sc=2.0*n/(S*S)
       if sc < best -1e-15:
          best=sc; best_n=n
    return best_n,best
```
Score for n=1 2; n maybe. Good.

`make_valid_list` for negative target sum: If after adding target, values exceed 1000? scale ensures sum1, values ~1/n so fine. If a contains inf, replace0. If all zeros and nonnegative, set uniform 1/n. If signed and all zeros, uniform.

```
def make_valid_list(a, nonnegative, target_sum=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return [1.0]
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    if nonnegative:
       a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
       s=a.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
          a=np.ones(a.size, dtype=float)/a.size
          s=1.0
       else:
          a=target_sum * a / s
          a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
          s=a.sum()
          if s<0.01: a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    else:
       a=np.clip(a,-1000.0,1000.0)
       s=a.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(s) or abs(s) < 0.01:
          a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size
          s=1.0
       else:
          if s < 0: a = -a; s=-s
          a=target_sum*a/s
          a=np.clip(a,-1000.0,1000.0)
          s=a.sum()
          if abs(s)<0.01: a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    # ensure at least one positive? for signed maybe sum target保证.
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

However scaling before clipping is not scale-invariant if clipping discards negatives: If you scale signed list to sum1, positives sum may be different than scaling after clipping. Example signed sum positive with negative values; scaling to sum1 reduces positives, clipping gives sum_pos <1; evaluator score =2n max(clipped²)/sum_pos². If nonnegative `make_valid_list` clips original a then scales to sum1, positive scale different. Are scores equal? The evaluator clips then does not rescale; scale invariance means for any scaled clipped list, score same. If original signed list has positives P, negatives N, signed sum S=P+N. make_valid negative scales by 1/S: positives P/S. Clipped sum=P/S. Evaluator score =2n max((P/S)^2 conv)/ (P/S)^2 =2n max(P conv)/P². Nonnegative make_valid clips original P, sum=P, scales to 1: P/P=1; score=2n max(P conv)/P². Same. Good. So score_pos=score_neg under clipping. Good.

Now, exact evaluate of signed under hidden no clip: sum is 1 (after make_valid). Good.

Let's ensure LP nonnegative sequences have sum positive; q positive. After normalization, sum positive.

Let's write final code. Need not include target? print.

Double-check `lp_direction` if `linprog` is None. `time_limit` option: If `linprog` uses highspy, option key maybe `'time_limit'`? In scipy, `options` for HiGHS accepts `time_limit`. If not, linprog may ignore or warn. We wrap with try; if exception, retry no timeout. However if option invalid, it may not throw but warn. okay.

`linprog` res.status: 0 optimal, 1 infeasible, 2 unbounded, 3 maxiter, 4 numerical. If 3 and feasible, accept. If 4 maybe x may still feasible; accept if feasible. So:
```
if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x'): return None
if res.status not in (0,3,4): return None
g=... 
```
Feasibility check with `convolution_full(a,g)` maybe expensive but okay. If max > 1+1e-4 scale; if after scale max>1+1e-3 return None.

Potential memory: In `lp_direction`, `A` dense may take memory; after function returns, freed? local. Good.

`optimize_nonnegative` after LP line search: `new, sc = line_search_state(a,b)` returns fast score. Then `new, sc_norm, _ = normalize_state(new)`; if sc_norm finite and improved. If line search found t=0, sc_norm equals current, no improvement, break.

Could line search with b from LP where G > S but full t=1 might violate max due g*g; ternary finds best. Good.

Block refine with q: Multiplicative update changes sum; score invariant if normalize? Multiplicative update not scale-invariant? If you multiply a block, score changes; no need sum fixed. `normalize_state` after accept scales to max1, score invariant under global scaling, okay. It may alter relative? Global scaling only, no relative. Good.

One more thought: `fast_score` for nonnegative q length n calculates max via FFT, which may be slightly >1 due numerical. `normalize_state` divides by that max; q LP constraints based on normalized state using same FFT, but exact evaluator direct may have max slightly different. Minor.

```python
"""
Optimizer for the coefficient sequence problem.

Main ideas
----------
1.  Use a strong analytic seed: coefficients of (1 - z)^(-1/2).
    For this sequence the first n-1 convolution coefficients are exactly 1,
    giving an O(sqrt(n)) sum and a score near pi/2 instead of 2.
2.  Improve such nonnegative seeds with a Frank-Wolfe style LP step:
        max sum(g)  s.t.  a * g <= 1, g >= 0
    followed by an exact convex line-search.
3.  Add a bounded random block-refinement phase.
4.  Also probe a few signed constructions (useful only if the evaluator does
    not apply the nonnegative clipping); under the shipped evaluator these are
    automatically reduced/compared to their clipped nonnegative versions.
5.  Always keep a valid fallback and return the best candidate found.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def safe_evaluate(seq):
    """Call the supplied evaluator, returning inf on any failure."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def convolution_full(x, y):
    """Full linear convolution of two 1-D sequences, using FFT when useful."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    nx = x.size
    ny = y.size
    if nx == 0 or ny == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    need = nx + ny - 1
    if need <= 128:
        return np.convolve(x, y)

    m = 1
    while m < need:
        m <<= 1

    fx = np.fft.fft(x, m)
    fy = np.fft.fft(y, m)
    return np.fft.ifft(fx * fy).real[:need]


def fast_score(a):
    """Fast scale-invariant score used for internal search."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return float("inf")

    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or abs(s) < 1e-300:
        return float("inf")

    c = convolution_full(a, a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    if m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0

    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def normalize_state(a):
    """
    Scale a to have max convolution coefficient 1 and return its score.
    The returned score is 2*n / sum(a)^2 for the normalized sequence.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return a, float("inf"), 0.0

    c = convolution_full(a, a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return a, float("inf"), 0.0

    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 1e-300:
        return a, float("inf"), float(a.sum())

    b = a / m
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
        return a, float("inf"), 0.0

    s = float(b.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or abs(s) < 1e-300:
        return b, float("inf"), s

    score = float(2.0 * n / (s * s))
    return b, score, s


def q_sequence(n):
    """
    Nonnegative seed: coefficients of (1 - z)^(-1/2).
    a_k = binom(2k,k) / 4^k.
    """
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * float(2 * i - 1) / float(2 * i)
    return a


def scan_q(max_n=4096):
    """
    For the q seed, max convolution is 1, so score = 2*n / S(n)^2.
    Find a good length in O(max_n).
    """
    if max_n < 1:
        return 1, 2.0

    a = np.empty(max_n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    s = 1.0

    best_n = 1
    best_score = 2.0

    for n in range(1, max_n + 1):
        idx = n - 1
        if n > 1:
            a[idx] = a[idx - 1] * float(2 * idx - 1) / float(2 * idx)
            s += float(a[idx])

        sc = float(2.0 * n / (s * s))
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_n = n

    return best_n, best_score


def _line_value(t, caa, cab, cbb, n, inv_s2):
    """Objective along a line between two same-sum sequences."""
    u = 1.0 - t
    c = (u * u) * caa + (t * u) * cab + (t * t) * cbb
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    if m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m * inv_s2)


def line_search_state(a, b):
    """
    Minimize score on the segment a -> b.  b is internally rescaled to the
    same sum as a, so the sum is constant.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    sa = float(a.sum())
    sb = float(b.sum())
    if sa <= 1e-300 or sb <= 1e-300:
        return a, fast_score(a)

    b = (sa / sb) * b
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
        return a, fast_score(a)

    caa = convolution_full(a, a)
    cab = convolution_full(a, b)
    cbb = convolution_full(b, b)
    inv_s2 = 1.0 / (sa * sa)

    best_t = 0.0
    best_f = fast_score(a)

    for t in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
        f = _line_value(t, caa, cab, cbb, n, inv_s2)
        if f < best_f:
            best_f = f
            best_t = t

    lo = 0.0
    hi = 1.0
    for _ in range(40):
        m1 = lo + (hi - lo) / 3.0
        m2 = hi - (hi - lo) / 3.0
        f1 = _line_value(m1, caa, cab, cbb, n, inv_s2)
        f2 = _line_value(m2, caa, cab, cbb, n, inv_s2)
        if f1 < f2:
            hi = m2
        else:
            lo = m1

    t = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    f = _line_value(t, caa, cab, cbb, n, inv_s2)
    if f < best_f:
        best_f = f
        best_t = t

    cand = (1.0 - best_t) * a + best_t * b
    cand = np.where(np.isfinite(cand), cand, a)
    return cand, best_f


def lp_direction(a, timeout_s=60.0):
    """
    Solve the Frank-Wolfe LP direction:
        max 1^T g
        s.t. a * g <= 1
             g >= 0
    The current sequence a is assumed to have max convolution coefficient 1.
    """
    n = a.size
    if n < 16 or n > 1536:
        return None
    if linprog is None:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    if np.any(a < -1e-12):
        return None

    A = np.empty((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(2 * n - 1):
        js = max(0, k - n + 1)
        je = min(n - 1, k)
        if js <= je:
            # Coefficients for j = js..je are a[k-j], i.e. descending indices.
            A[k, js:je + 1] = a[k - js : k - je - 1 : -1]

    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0e6) for _ in range(n)]

    res = None
    timeout_s = max(1.0, float(timeout_s))

    try:
        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True, "time_limit": int(timeout_s)}
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(
                c_obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True}
            )
        except Exception:
            return None

    if res is None or not hasattr(res, "x"):
        return None
    if res.status not in (0, 3, 4):
        return None

    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if g.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return None

    g = np.clip(g, 0.0, None)
    gs = float(g.sum())
    if gs <= 1e-12:
        return None

    # Verify feasibility with the same fast convolution used elsewhere.
    try:
        ag = convolution_full(a, g)
        if ag.size > 0:
            mg = float(np.max(ag))
            if np.isfinite(mg) and mg > 1.0 + 1e-6:
                scale = (1.0 + 1e-8) / mg
                if scale < 1.0:
                    g *= max(scale, 0.0)
                else:
                    return None
    except Exception:
        return None

    return g


def refine_blocks(a, score, deadline, seed=12345):
    """
    Lightweight multiplicative block search.  This is intentionally bounded
    and always returns a valid nonnegative approximation if possible.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return a, float("inf")

    if deadline is None:
        return a, score
    deadline = float(deadline)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 1.0:
        return a, score

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    best_score = fast_score(best)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        best_score = fast_score(best)

    step = 0.05
    no_improve = 0
    attempts = 0
    max_attempts = 800 if n > 1000 else 2500

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and attempts < max_attempts:
        wmin = max(1, n // 200)
        wmax = max(wmin, n // 8)
        wmax = min(n, max(wmin + 1, wmax))

        for _ in range(10):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline or attempts >= max_attempts:
                break

            width = int(rng.integers(wmin, wmax + 1))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))

            cand = best.copy()
            factors = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, step, width))
            cand[start:start + width] *= factors

            if rng.random() < 0.25:
                cand = 0.5 * (cand + cand[::-1])

            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
            sc = fast_score(cand)

            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                cand, sc, _ = normalize_state(cand)
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
                sc = fast_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = cand
                    best_score = sc
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
            else:
                no_improve += 1

            attempts += 1

        if no_improve > 1500:
            step = min(0.6, step * 1.4)
            no_improve = 0
        if step > 0.55:
            break

    return best, best_score


def optimize_nonnegative(n, deadline):
    """
    Optimize a nonnegative sequence of length n starting from the q seed.
    deadline is an absolute monotonic time.
    """
    if n < 4:
        n = 4

    try:
        a = q_sequence(n)
        a, score, _ = normalize_state(a)
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            a, score, _ = normalize_state(a)
    except Exception:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        a, score, _ = normalize_state(a)

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    if deadline is None or time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        max_iter = 6 if n <= 512 else 3
        for _ in range(max_iter):
            if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2.0:
                break

            remaining = max(10.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic() - 5.0)
            timeout_s = min(90.0, remaining)
            if n <= 350:
                timeout_s = min(timeout_s, 35.0)
            elif n <= 800:
                timeout_s = min(timeout_s, 55.0)

            try:
                g = lp_direction(a, timeout_s=timeout_s)
            except Exception:
                g = None

            if g is None:
                break

            G = float(g.sum())
            S = float(a.sum())
            if G <= S * 1.0005:
                break

            b = (S / G) * g
            b = np.clip(b, 0.0, None)

            try:
                cand, cand_score = line_search_state(a, b)
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
                cand, cand_score, _ = normalize_state(cand)
            except Exception:
                break

            if np.isfinite(cand_score) and cand_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = cand
                best_score = cand_score
                a = cand

    # Small amount of local polishing.
    if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 3.0:
        block_deadline = min(float(deadline), time.monotonic() + 25.0)
        try:
            best, best_score = refine_blocks(best, best_score, block_deadline, seed=9101)
        except Exception:
            pass

    return best, best_score


def signed_anti_candidate(n, rng, deadline):
    """
    Cheap signed heuristic: anti-palindromic +/-1 patterns plus an amplitude
    parameter.  Under the shipped nonnegative evaluator this will normally be
    clipped and compared to its nonnegative version.
    """
    if n < 12 or rng is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    half = n // 2

    max_restarts = 70 if n <= 160 else 35
    base_alphas = (0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0)

    for _ in range(max_restarts):
        if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline - 1.0:
            break

        p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(half):
            s = 1.0 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1.0
            p[i] = s
            p[n - 1 - i] = -s

        local_score = float("inf")
        local_alpha = 1.0
        local_p = p

        for alpha in base_alphas:
            for ps in (p, -p):
                a = (1.0 + alpha * ps) / float(n)
                sc = fast_score(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < local_score:
                    local_score = sc
                    local_alpha = alpha
                    local_p = ps.copy()
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best = a.copy()

        # Local sign-flip polish.
        for _fl in range(35):
            if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline - 1.0:
                break
            if half <= 0:
                break

            i = int(rng.integers(0, half))
            local_p[i] *= -1.0
            local_p[n - 1 - i] *= -1.0

            if local_alpha == 0.0:
                alpha_list = (0.0, 0.5, 1.0)
            else:
                alpha_list = (0.6 * local_alpha, local_alpha, 1.4 * local_alpha)

            for alpha in alpha_list:
                a = (1.0 + alpha * local_p) / float(n)
                sc = fast_score(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < local_score:
                    local_score = sc
                    local_alpha = alpha
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best = a.copy()

    if best is not None:
        best, _, _ = normalize_state(best)
    return best, best_score


def signed_smooth_candidate(n, rng, deadline):
    """
    Small smooth signed subgradient search.  It is optional and time-bounded.
    """
    if n < 32 or rng is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    max_restarts = 5
    max_iter = 2200

    for _restart in range(max_restarts):
        if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2.0:
            break

        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

        b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for _ in range(4):
            freq = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 0.5 * n))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
            b += np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * freq * idx / float(n) + phase)

        b -= b.mean()
        nb = float(np.linalg.norm(b))
        if nb > 1e-12:
            a += (0.5 / nb) * b
            a -= a.mean() - 1.0 / float(n)

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
            continue

        m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        for it in range(max_iter):
            if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline - 1.0:
                break

            try:
                c = convolution_full(a, a)
                maxc = float(np.max(c))
            except Exception:
                break

            if c.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(maxc) or maxc <= 0.0:
                break

            tau = max(abs(maxc) * 0.12, 1e-12)
            z = (c - maxc) / tau
            z = z - float(np.max(z))
            w = np.exp(z)
            ws = float(np.sum(w))

            if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
                w = np.zeros_like(c)
                w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
            else:
                w /= ws

            try:
                grad = 2.0 * np.correlate(w, a, mode="valid")
            except Exception:
                grad = None

            if grad is None or grad.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
                k = int(np.argmax(c))
                grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                for i in range(n):
                    j = k - i
                    if 0 <= j < n:
                        grad[i] = 2.0 * a[j]

            # Keep the sum constraint tangent: sum(delta) = 0.
            grad -= float(np.mean(grad))

            m = 0.90 * m + 0.10 * grad
            v = 0.99 * v + 0.01 * (grad * grad)

            denom = np.sqrt(v) + 1e-12
            delta = m / denom
            dmean = float(np.mean(np.abs(delta)))
            amean = float(np.mean(np.abs(a)))
            scale = 0.2 * amean / max(dmean, 1e-15)
            scale = float(min(scale, 1.0e6))

            newa = a - scale * delta
            newa -= float(np.mean(newa)) - 1.0 / float(n)
            newa = np.where(np.isfinite(newa), newa, a)

            sc = fast_score(newa)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best = newa.copy()

            a = newa

    if best is not None:
        best, _, _ = normalize_state(best)
    return best, best_score


def make_valid_list(a, nonnegative, target_sum=1.0):
    """
    Convert an array to a list suitable for the evaluator.  The sequence is
    scaled to have positive sum 1 (unless that is impossible).
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return [1.0]

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)

    if nonnegative:
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
        else:
            a = target_sum * a / s
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(a.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
                a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    else:
        a = np.clip(a, -1000.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if (not np.isfinite(s)) or abs(s) < 0.01:
            a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
            s = 1.0
        else:
            if s < 0.0:
                a = -a
                s = -s
            a = target_sum * a / s
            a = np.clip(a, -1000.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(a.sum())
            if abs(s) < 0.01:
                a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)

    return [float(x) for x in a]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Return the best coefficient sequence found under the time budget.
    The function always returns a nonempty list of finite numbers.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        total_budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        total_budget = 2400.0

    if not np.isfinite(total_budget) or total_budget <= 0:
        total_budget = 2400.0

    # Keep a few seconds for final exact evaluation and return overhead.
    deadline = start + max(60.0, total_budget - 25.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    candidates = []
    seen_lengths = set()

    def add_candidate(a):
        try:
            a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if a.size <= 0:
                return
            a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
            candidates.append(a)
            seen_lengths.add(int(a.size))
        except Exception:
            return

    # 1) Analytic q seeds.
    try:
        q_best_n, q_best_score = scan_q(4096)
        print(f"[q] scan best length={q_best_n} score={q_best_score:.10f}")
    except Exception:
        q_best_n, q_best_score = 1024, 2.0

    for n in (16, 64, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, int(q_best_n)):
        if n < 4:
            n = 4
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 5.0:
            break
        try:
            add_candidate(q_sequence(int(n)))
        except Exception:
            pass

    # 2) Nonnegative LP/local optimization at a few sizes.
    lp_jobs = (
        (256, 80.0),
        (512, 140.0),
        (1024, 260.0),
    )

    for n, tlim in lp_jobs:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 180.0:
            break
        lim = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + tlim)
        try:
            a, sc = optimize_nonnegative(int(n), lim)
            if a is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(a)) and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(a)
                print(f"[nn-LP] n={n:5d} score={sc:.10f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    # One larger nonnegative pass if enough time remains.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 350.0:
        try:
            lim = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 260.0)
            a, sc = optimize_nonnegative(2048, lim)
            if a is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(a)) and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(a)
                print(f"[nn-LP-large] n=2048 score={sc:.10f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    # 3) Optional signed probes.  Under the shipped evaluator these are
    #    compared against their clipped nonnegative equivalents below.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 45.0:
        for n in (128, 256):
            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
                lim = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 35.0)
                try:
                    a, sc = signed_anti_candidate(int(n), rng, lim)
                    if a is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(a)) and np.isfinite(sc):
                        add_candidate(a)
                        print(f"[signed-anti] n={n:3d} score={sc:.10f}")
                except Exception:
                    pass

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        lim = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 90.0)
        try:
            a, sc = signed_smooth_candidate(128, rng, lim)
            if a is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(a)) and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(a)
                print(f"[signed-smooth] n=128 score={sc:.10f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    # 4) If the old parent sequence exists and is not too large, include it.
    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if parent is not None:
            p = np.clip(np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel(), 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.size >= 1 and p.size <= 2048 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
                add_candidate(p)
                if time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
                    try:
                        pn, psc = normalize_state(p)
                        pn = np.clip(pn, 0.0, 1000.0)
                        pn, psc = refine_blocks(pn, psc, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 20.0), seed=555)
                        add_candidate(pn)
                    except Exception:
                        pass
    except Exception:
        pass

    # 5) Pick the best exact candidate.
    best_seq = [1.0, 1.0]
    best_score = safe_evaluate(best_seq)

    # Always include a very safe explicit fallback.
    add_candidate(np.ones(8, dtype=np.float64))

    for a in candidates:
        try:
            a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if a.size <= 0:
                continue

            # Nonnegative clipped/scaled version.
            seq_pos = make_valid_list(a, True, 1.0)
            score_pos = safe_evaluate(seq_pos)

            if np.isfinite(score_pos) and score_pos < best_score:
                best_score = score_pos
                best_seq = seq_pos

            # If the array contains negative values, also test it as-is.
            if np.any(a < -1e-12):
                seq_neg = make_valid_list(a, False, 1.0)
                score_neg = safe_evaluate(seq_neg)

                if np.isfinite(score_neg) and score_neg < best_score:
                    # If the evaluator clips, score_pos should be the same or
                    # better; then keep the safely nonnegative sequence.
                    if np.isfinite(score_pos) and score_pos <= score_neg + 1e-10:
                        best_score = score_pos
                        best_seq = seq_pos
                    else:
                        best_score = score_neg
                        best_seq = seq_neg
        except Exception:
            continue

    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_score = safe_evaluate(best_seq)

    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_seq = [1.0, 1.0]
        best_score = safe_evaluate(best_seq)

    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_seq = [1.0, 1.0]

    print(f"[final] length={len(best_seq)} score={best_score}")
    return best_seq
```
